Cedar is the real-time audio\nengine of nkido: a bytecode VM that processes blocks of 128 float samples at\n48 kHz, zero allocations on the audio path. The repository is your current\nworking directory.\n\n## Task\n\nMake these opcodes faster on **x86-64 AVX2** (the gate machine):\n\n- `op_distort_tanh`\n- `op_distort_soft`\n\nThey live in `cedar/include/cedar/opcodes/distortion.hpp`. Current source of the opcode bodies:\n\n```cpp\n[[gnu::always_inline]]\ninline void op_distort_tanh(ExecutionContext& ctx, const Instruction& inst) {\n    float* out_l = ctx.buffers->get(inst.out_buffer);\n    float* out_r = ctx.buffers->get(static_cast<std::uint16_t>(inst.out_buffer + 1));\n    const float* input = ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[0]);\n    const bool stereo_in = (inst.flags & InstructionFlag::STEREO_INPUT) != 0;\n    const float* input_r = stereo_in\n        ? ctx.buffers->get(static_cast<std::uint16_t>(inst.inputs[0] + 1))\n        : nullptr;\n    const float* drive = ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[1]);\n    const float* dry_level = (inst.inputs[2] != 0xFFFF) ? ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[2]) : nullptr;\n    const float* wet_level = (inst.inputs[3] != 0xFFFF) ? ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[3]) : nullptr;\n\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; ++i) {\n        float d = std::max(0.1f, drive[i]);\n        float dry = drywet::coeff(dry_level, i, 0.0f);\n        float wet = drywet::coeff(wet_level, i, 1.0f);\n        float x_l = input[i];\n        float x_r = stereo_in ? input_r[i] : x_l;\n        out_l[i] = drywet::mix(x_l, std::tanh(x_l * d), dry, wet);\n        out_r[i] = drywet::mix(x_r, std::tanh(x_r * d), dry, wet);\n    }\n}\n\n[[gnu::always_inline]]\ninline void op_distort_soft(ExecutionContext& ctx, const Instruction& inst) {\n    float* out_l = ctx.buffers->get(inst.out_buffer);\n    float* out_r = ctx.buffers->get(static_cast<std::uint16_t>(inst.out_buffer + 1));\n    const float* input = ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[0]);\n    const bool stereo_in = (inst.flags & InstructionFlag::STEREO_INPUT) != 0;\n    const float* input_r = stereo_in\n        ? ctx.buffers->get(static_cast<std::uint16_t>(inst.inputs[0] + 1))\n        : nullptr;\n    const float* threshold = ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[1]);\n    const float* dry_level = (inst.inputs[2] != 0xFFFF) ? ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[2]) : nullptr;\n    const float* wet_level = (inst.inputs[3] != 0xFFFF) ? ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[3]) : nullptr;\n\n    auto soft = [](float x) {\n        if (x > 3.0f) return 1.0f;\n        if (x < -3.0f) return -1.0f;\n        float x2 = x * x;\n        return x * (27.0f + x2) / (27.0f + 9.0f * x2);\n    };\n\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; ++i) {\n        float t = std::clamp(threshold[i], 0.1f, 2.0f);\n        float dry = drywet::coeff(dry_level, i, 0.0f);\n        float wet = drywet::coeff(wet_level, i, 1.0f);\n        float x_l = input[i];\n        float x_r = stereo_in ? input_r[i] : x_l;\n        out_l[i] = drywet::mix(x_l, soft(x_l / t) * t, dry, wet);\n        out_r[i] = drywet::mix(x_r, soft(x_r / t) * t, dry, wet);\n    }\n}\n```\n\nCost on the gate machine (median ns per opcode-block, lower is better):\n\n| Opcode | Original scalar | Current (accepted so far) | Speedup so far |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `op_distort_tanh` | 974.44 | 974.44 | 1.00x |\n| `op_distort_soft` | 257.33 | 257.33 | 1.00x |\n\n## Accepted so far (already in the tree)\n\nNothing yet \u2014 the tree is the original scalar code.\n\n## How to add a kernel\n\nThe dispatch header `cedar/include/cedar/dsp/simd.hpp` is already there. Keep\nthe scalar body and add a kernel in front of it:\n\n```cpp\n#include \"../dsp/simd.hpp\"\n\nnamespace kernels {\n#if CEDAR_SIMD_X86\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void mul_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8)\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i, _mm256_mul_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\n}\n#endif\n}\n\ninline void op_mul(ExecutionContext& ctx, const Instruction& inst) {\n    // ... resolve a, b, out ...\n#if CEDAR_SIMD_X86\n    if (simd::has(simd::Isa::Avx2)) { kernels::mul_avx2(a, b, out); return; }\n#endif\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; ++i) out[i] = a[i] * b[i];  // fallback stays\n}\n```\n\n- `CEDAR_TARGET_AVX2` is `[[gnu::target(\"avx2\")]]`; no compiler flags change.\n  FMA is **not** enabled (it would change rounding).\n- Buffers from `ctx.buffers->get()` are 32-byte aligned; `BLOCK_SIZE` is 128.\n- You may also add NEON (`CEDAR_SIMD_NEON`) or WASM SIMD128\n  (`CEDAR_SIMD_WASM`) paths; they are measured later on other machines, not\n  gated here.\n\n## Rules (enforced by the verifier \u2014 violations are rejected automatically)\n\n1. static_cast<int>(x);\n   173\t    if (x < -0.5f) x += 1.0f;\n   174\t    else if (x > 0.5f) x -= 1.0f;\n   175\t\n   176\t    // Parabolic approximation\n   177\t    float y = 4.0f * x * (1.0f - std::abs(x));\n   178\t    // Refinement for better accuracy\n   179\t    return y * (0.775f + 0.225f * std::abs(y));\n   180\t}\n   181\t\n   182\t// ============================================================================\n   183\t// LFO Shapes (for modulation effects)\n   184\t// ============================================================================\n   185\t\n   186\t// Triangle wave from phase (0-1)\n   187\t[[gnu::always_inline]]\n   188\tinline float lfo_triangle(float phase) {\n   189\t    return 4.0f * std::abs(phase - 0.5f) - 1.0f;\n   190\t}\n   191\t\n   192\t// Sine wave from phase (0-1)\n   193\t[[gnu::always_inline]]\n   194\tinline float lfo_sine(float phase) {\n   195\t    return std::sin(phase * TWO_PI);\n   196\t}\n   197\t\n   198\t}  // namespace cedar"

A speedup inside that band is not a speedup.\n   253\t- Records CPU model and scaling governor; refuses to gate (warns and marks the\n   254\t  run `unstable`) if the governor is not `performance`.\n   255\t\n   256\t### 5.4 Log schema\n   257\t\n   258\tPer iteration, appended to `runs/<run-id>/iterations.jsonl`:\n   259\t\n   260\t```json\n   261\t{\"run_id\":\"2026-09-28T14:03Z_opus5_op_distort_tanh\",\"iteration\":3,\n   262\t \"model\":\"claude-opus-5\",\"provider\":\"claude-code\",\n   263\t \"opcode\":\"op_distort_tanh\",\"isa_target\":\"avx2\",\n   264\t \"tokens\":{\"input\":18422,\"output\":2811,\"cached\":12000},\n   265\t \"cost_usd\":0.41,\"wall_clock_s\":96.3,\"gpu_s\":null,\n   266\t \"verdict\":\"accept\",\"reject_reason\":null,\n   267\t \"gates\":{\"build\":\"pass\",\"equality\":\"approx\",\"null_test_dbfs\":-112.4,\n   268\t          \"unit\":\"pass\",\"zero_alloc\":\"pass\",\"experiment\":\"pass\"},\n   269\t \"bench\":{\"baseline_ns\":118.7,\"candidate_ns\":41.9,\"speedup\":2.83,\"noise_band\":0.031},\n   270\t \"patch_sha\":\"...\",\"branch\":\"autoresearch/opus5-distort-tanh\"}\n   271\t```\n   272\t\n   273\tPer run, `runs/<run-id>/summary.json`: totals (tokens, USD, wall clock, GPU\n   274\tseconds), best speedup, iteration index of first accept, accept/reject counts.\n   275\t\n   276\t### 5.5 Anti-cheat\n   277\t\n   278\tThe loop measures the thing it optimises, so the model must not be able to touch\n   279\tthe measurement. `verify.sh` enforces a **write allowlist**:\n   280\t\n   281\t- Writable: `cedar/include/cedar/opcodes/**`, `cedar/include/cedar/dsp/simd*.hpp`,\n   282\t  `cedar/src/dsp/simd*.cpp`.\n   283\t- Everything else \u2014 benchmarks, tests, experiments, budgets, CMake \u2014 is restored\n   284\t  from a pristine checkout before every verify. A patch touching them is an\n   285\t  automatic reject, logged as `reject_reason: \"out-of-allowlist edit\"`.\n   286\t\n   287\tThis is also a talk beat: the first thing you defend against is the optimiser\n   288\toptimising the scoreboard.\n   289\t\n   290\t---\n   291\t\n   292\t## 6. Edge Cases\n   293\t\n   294\t1. **Benchmark noise / thermal drift.** Long runs heat the machine and slow late\n   295\t   iterations. Mitigation: interleave baseline re-measurement every iteration\n   296\t   (speedup is always ratio-to-a-fresh-baseline, never to a number from an hour\n   297\t   ago); mark runs `unstable` when the fresh baseline drifts > 10 % from the\n   298\t   pinned one.\n   299\t2. **Model edits the benchmark or the tests.** Rejected by the allowlist (\u00a75.5).\n   300\t3. **Patch does not compile.** Counts as an iteration; compiler output is fed\n   301\t   back as the rejection reason.\n   302\t4. **Bit-identity impossible** (`tanh`, `tan`, `pow`, FP reassociation). Falls to\n   303\t   the tolerance path; requires the declared `// simd: approx` comment.\n   304\t5. **Approximation passes the null test but changes musical behaviour** \u2014 e.g. a\n   305\t   `tanh` approximation that alters a filter's self-oscillation. Caught by the\n   306\t   winners-only listening pass; if it fails there, the change is rejected even\n   307\t   though every automated gate was green.\n   308\t6. **SIMD is slower.** Expected for C4 (feedback-serial). Recorded as a result;\n   309\t   the loop stops nominating that shape after two consecutive losses.\n   310\t7. **Local model too weak to produce a valid patch in 8 iterations.** Recorded as\n   311\t   zero accepted changes with its token cost \u2014 that is a finding for the talk,\n   312\t   not a reason to give it extra iterations.\n   313\t8. **Local model context window too small** for the opcode file. Recorded as a\n   314\t   capability gap; the prompt may extract just the function body, and the fact\n   315\t   that this was needed is reported.\n   316\t9. **Accepted change breaks a different opcode.** Full `cedar_tests` runs every\n   317\t   iteration, so this fails the gate; if it escapes, the winners-only memory\n   318\t   harness pass is the backstop.\n   319\t10. **Two runs collide on the same files.** One git worktree per run; runs are\n   320\t    serialised on the benchmark machine anyway (concurrent benching is invalid).\n   321\t11. **Runaway spend.** Fixed iteration count bounds it, but the driver also hard-\n   322\t    stops a run at **$20**, logging the run as `budget-stopped`.\n   323\t12. **Denormals.** A kernel that flushes denormals where the scalar path does not\n   324\t    changes output. FTZ/DAZ state must be identical in both builds; the A/B test\n   325\t    is run with the engine's normal settings.\n   326\t\n   327\t---\n   328\t\n   329\t## 7. Impact Assessment\n   330\t\n   331\t| Component | Status | Notes |\n   332\t|---|---|---|\n   333\t| `cedar/include/cedar/opcodes/*` (candidates) | **Modified** | Kernels added; scalar bodies kept intact |\n   334\t| All other opcodes | **Stays** | Untouched this round |\n   335\t| `cedar/include/cedar/vm/buffer_pool.hpp` | **Stays** | Already 32-byte aligned |\n   336\t| VM dispatch (`cedar/src/vm/vm.cpp`) | **Stays** | No instrumentation added to the hot switch |\n   337\t| `CMakePresets.json`, `cmake/CompilerOptions.cmake` | **Modified** | New `simd` build option + WASM `-msimd128`; default baselines unchanged |\n   338\t| `cedar/benchmarks/` | **New** | `cedar_bench` target |\n   339\t| `cedar/include/cedar/dsp/simd.hpp` | **New** | ISA detection + kernel declarations |"

I will review and commit manually.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:1: ---\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:2: name: update-changelog\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:3: description: Use when asked to update the CHANGELOG, prepare release notes, or write changelog entries for a new version of nkido. Drafts categorized entries from recent commits in Keep a Changelog format and writes them to CHANGELOG.md.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:4: ---\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:6: # Update Changelog\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:8: ## Purpose\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:10: Draft a CHANGELOG.md section for an upcoming nkido release. The CHANGELOG is\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:11: the single source of truth for release notes \u2014 the GitHub Release body is\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:12: extracted from it on tag push by `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, so the\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:13: quality of this entry directly determines what users see on GitHub.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:15: ## Conventions (do not break)\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:17: - Format: [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:18:   already established at the top of `CHANGELOG.md`.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:19: - Categories (in this order, omit empty ones):\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:20:   `Added`, `Changed`, `Deprecated`, `Removed`, `Fixed`, `Security`.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:21: - Versioned heading format: `## [X.Y.Z] - YYYY-MM-DD`.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:22: - Newest version on top, directly below the preamble.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:23: - `VERSION` file is the semver source of truth. `web/package.json` is kept\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:24:   in sync by `scripts/bump-version.sh` \u2014 do not edit it here.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:26: ## Inputs you must gather first\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:28: Run these (in parallel when possible) before drafting anything:\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:30: 1. `cat VERSION` \u2014 the current released version.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:31: 2. `git tag --list 'v*' | sort -V | tail -5` \u2014 recent tags.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:32: 3. `git log <last-tag>..HEAD --oneline` \u2014 commits since the last release.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:33: 4. `head -40 CHANGELOG.md` \u2014 confirms format and shows whether an\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:34:    `## [Unreleased]` or already-versioned section is in flight.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:36: ## Mode detection\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:38: After reading CHANGELOG.md, decide which mode you are in:\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:40: - **Accrual mode** \u2014 no in-progress section exists. Insert a new\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:41:   `## [Unreleased]` block above the most recent versioned heading and fill\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:42:   it with categorized entries derived from commits since the last tag.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:43: - **Finalize mode** \u2014 an `## [Unreleased]` block exists and the user has\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:44:   asked to cut a release (e.g. \"finalize 0.1.2\", \"release patch\"). Compute\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:45:   the next version (`bump-version.sh` logic: major resets minor+patch,\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:46:   minor resets patch, patch increments) and rename `[Unreleased]` to\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:47:   `[X.Y.Z] - YYYY-MM-DD` using today's date. Add or refine entries from any\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:48:   commits the Unreleased section is missing.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:49: - **Direct-versioned mode** \u2014 user explicitly asks for a specific version\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:50:   number (e.g. \"write a 0.1.2 entry\"). Skip Unreleased; insert\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:51:   `## [X.Y.Z] - YYYY-MM-DD` directly.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:53: If unsure, ask the user which mode (one short question, then proceed).\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:55: ## Drafting rules\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:57: - Read each commit's message and (if the subject is terse) `git show --stat`\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:58:   the commit to understand scope.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:59: - Group commits by intent, not by author or chronology:\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:60:   - **Added**: new opcodes, builtins, UI components, public APIs, files.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:61:   - **Changed**: rewrites/behavioral changes to existing features. UI\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:62:     redesigns belong here unless purely additive.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:63:   - **Fixed**: bug fixes, crash fixes, off-by-ones, regressions.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:64:   - **Removed**: deleted opcodes, retired flags, dropped browser support.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:65:   - **Deprecated**: still works but slated for removal.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:66:   - **Security**: vulnerabilities and CVE-class fixes.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:67: - One bullet per user-visible change, not per commit. Squash several\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:68:   commits into one bullet when they describe one thing (e.g. \"Add chorus\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:69:   effect\" + \"Tune chorus depth defaults\" \u2192 single Added bullet).\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:70: - Skip pure-internal commits (refactors with no observable effect, comment\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:71:   fixes, CI tweaks, doc-only changes that do not change usage). When in\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:72:   doubt, omit \u2014 the changelog is for users, not contributors.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:73: - Bullet voice: imperative or noun-phrase, matching the existing 0.1.0\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:74:   entries (e.g. \"Hot-swap live coding with crossfade transitions\").\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:75: - Reference user-facing concepts (opcode names, UI panels, language\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:76:   features) over file paths.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:78: ## Confirmation step\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:80: Before writing to CHANGELOG.md:\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:82: 1. Show the user the proposed entries as a markdown block in chat.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:83: 2. Mention any commits you intentionally skipped, in one line each, so the\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:84:    user can object if you dropped something they consider user-visible.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:85: 3. Wait for approval (or edits) before saving.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:87: ## Writing to CHANGELOG.md\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:89: - Use the `Edit` tool, not `Write` \u2014 preserve the preamble and existing\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:90:   versioned sections exactly.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:91: - Insert the new section directly under the preamble (above the previous\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:92:   newest version).\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:93: - Leave one blank line between sections.\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:95: ## After saving\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:97: Tell the user the next steps in one short message:\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:99: ```\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:100: Review:  git diff CHANGELOG.md\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:101: Bump:    ./scripts/bump-version.sh <major|minor|patch>\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:102: Push:    git push origin master --tags\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:103: ```\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:105: The bump script will refuse to proceed if `## [X.Y.Z]` is missing, so the\n.claude/skills/update-changelog/SKILL.md:106: order matters: changelog first, then bump, then push. Buffers come\\n// from the 32-byte aligned pool slabs, so the aligned load/store intrinsics\\n// are safe.\\n// ============================================================================\\nnamespace kernels {\\n#if CEDAR_SIMD_X86\\n\\nstatic_assert(BLOCK_SIZE % 8 == 0, \\\"AVX2 kernels need a multiple-of-8 block\\\");\\n\\n// Per-8-lane dry/wet mix coefficient: control-buffer read, or the sentinel\\n// fallback constant drywet::coeff() uses for an unconnected (0xFFFF) slot.\\ntemplate <bool FromBuf>\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline __m256 coeff_avx2([[maybe_unused]] const float* buf,\\n                                           [[maybe_unused]] std::size_t i, float fallback) noexcept {\\n    if constexpr (FromBuf) {\\n        return _mm256_load_ps(buf + i);\\n    } else {\\n        return _mm256_set1_ps(fallback);\\n    }\\n}\\n\\n// simd: approx std::tanh \u2014 tanh(x) = 1 - 2/(1 + e^(2x)), with e^(2x) split into\\n// an exact power of two and a degree-8 polynomial for 2^s on s in [0,1).\\n// Peak |error| is ~1e-7 (about -140 dBFS), inside the -100 dBFS budget this\\n// opcode is allowed for its declared approximation; every other operation in\\n// the opcode matches the scalar body bit for bit.\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline __m256 tanh_avx2(__m256 x) noexcept {\\n    const __m256 one = _mm256_set1_ps(1.0f);\\n    // z = 2*log2(e)*x, clamped into the normal-exponent range. tanh() has\\n    // saturated to +-1 long before that, so the clamp only touches samples\\n    // whose result is exactly +-1 anyway (and keeps the range reduction\\n    // well defined for |x| up to infinity).\\n    __m256 z = _mm256_mul_ps(x, _mm256_set1_ps(2.885390081777926814f));\\n    z = _mm256_max_ps(z, _mm256_set1_ps(-126.0f));\\n    z = _mm256_min_ps(z, _mm256_set1_ps(126.0f));\\n    // z = k + s with s in [0,1)  ->  2^z = 2^k * 2^s (exact split)\\n    const __m256i k = _mm256_cvttps_epi32(z);\\n    const __m256 s = _mm256_sub_ps(z, _mm256_cvtepi32_ps(k));\\n    // 2^s on [0,1): Taylor series around zero, truncation error <= 1.1e-7\\n    // relative (coefficients (ln2)^n / n!).\\n    __m256 p = _mm256_set1_ps(1.3215e-6f);\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), _mm256_set1_ps(1.5252734e-5f));\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), _mm256_set1_ps(1.5403531e-4f));\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), _mm256_set1_ps(1.3333559e-3f));\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), _mm256_set1_ps(9.6181291e-3f));\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), _mm256_set1_ps(5.5504109e-2f));\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), _mm256_set1_ps(2.4022651e-1f));\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), _mm256_set1_ps(6.9314718e-1f));\\n    p = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(p, s), one);\\n    const __m256 scale = _mm256_castsi256_ps(\\n        _mm256_slli_epi32(_mm256_add_epi32(k, _mm256_set1_epi32(127)), 23));\\n    const __m256 e = _mm256_mul_ps(p, scale);\\n    return _mm256_sub_ps(one, _mm256_div_ps(_mm256_set1_ps(2.0f), _mm256_add_ps(one, e)));\\n}\\n\\n// soft() from op_distort_soft over 8 lanes. Same operation order as the scalar\\n// expression, so the |q| <= 3 lanes are bit-identical; the early-outs become\\n// selects that return the same exact +-1 the branches return (the polynomial\\n// is monotone and passes exactly through (3, 1) and (-3, -1)).\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline __m256 soft_avx2(__m256 x, __m256 t) noexcept {\\n    const __m256 q = _mm256_div_ps(x, t);  // x / t\\n    const __m256 q2 = _mm256_mul_ps(q, q);\\n    __m256 w = _mm256_div_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(q, _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_set1_ps(27.0f), q2)),\\n                             _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_set1_ps(27.0f),\\n                                           _mm256_mul_ps(_mm256_set1_ps(9.0f), q2)));\\n    w = _mm256_blendv_ps(w, _mm256_set1_ps(1.0f),\\n                         _mm256_cmp_ps(q, _mm256_set1_ps(3.0f), _CMP_GT_OQ));\\n    w = _mm256_blendv_ps(w, _mm256_set1_ps(-1.0f),\\n                         _mm256_cmp_ps(q, _mm256_set1_ps(-3.0f), _CMP_LT_OQ));\\n    return _mm256_mul_ps(w, t);  // soft(x / t) * t\\n}\\n\\n// DISTORT_TANH body over 8 lanes. `in_r` is only read when Stereo is set; a\\n// mono input broadcasts the L result to R, exactly like the scalar body.\\ntemplate <bool Stereo, bool HasDry, bool HasWet>\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 void distort_tanh_avx2(const float* in_l, const float* in_r, const float* drive,\\n                                         const float* dry_level, const float* wet_level, float* out_l,\\n                                         float* out_r) noexcept {\\n    const __m256 drive_floor = _mm256_set1_ps(0.1f);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        const __m256 d = _mm256_max_ps(_mm256_load_ps(drive + i), drive_floor);\\n        const __m256 dry = coeff_avx2<HasDry>(dry_level, i, 0.0f);\\n        const __m256 wet = coeff_avx2<HasWet>(wet_level, i, 1.0f);\\n        const __m256 x = _mm256_load_ps(in_l + i);\\n        const __m256 sat = tanh_avx2(_mm256_mul_ps(x, d));\\n        const __m256 res = _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(x, dry), _mm256_mul_ps(sat, wet));\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out_l + i, res);\\n        if constexpr (Stereo) {\\n            const __m256 xr = _mm256_load_ps(in_r + i);\\n            const __m256 sat_r = tanh_avx2(_mm256_mul_ps(xr, d));\\n            _mm256_store_ps(out_r + i, _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(xr, dry), _mm256_mul_ps(sat_r, wet)));\\n        } else {\\n            _mm256_store_ps(out_r + i, res);\\n        }\\n    }\\n}\\n\\n// DISTORT_SOFT body over 8 lanes, same story as above.\\ntemplate <bool Stereo, bool HasDry, bool HasWet>\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 void distort_soft_avx2(const float* in_l, const float* in_r, const float* threshold,\\n                                         const float* dry_level, const float* wet_level, float* out_l,\\n                                         float* out_r) noexcept {\\n    const __m256 lo = _mm256_set1_ps(0.1f);\\n    const __m256 hi = _mm256_set1_ps(2.0f);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[2]) : nullptr;\n   243\t    const float* wet_level = (inst.inputs[3] != 0xFFFF) ? ctx.buffers->get(inst.inputs[3]) : nullptr;\n   244\t\n   245\t    auto soft = [](float x) {\n   246\t        if (x > 3.0f) return 1.0f;\n   247\t        if (x < -3.0f) return -1.0f;\n   248\t        float x2 = x * x;\n   249\t        return x * (27.0f + x2) / (27.0f + 9.0f * x2);\n   250\t    };"

Replaced with an AVX2 kernel computing `tanh(x) = 1 - 2/(1+e^{2x})`, where `e^{2x}` is split into an exact power of two (integer exponent, exponent-bias trick) and a degree-8 polynomial for `2^s` on `s\u00e2\u0088\u0088[0,1)`. Marked `// simd: approx std::tanh`; measured peak error **-133.6 dBFS**, far inside the -100 dBFS budget.\n\n2. **`op_distort_soft`** \u2014 the bottleneck was per-sample branchy scalar code (two compares + early-outs) plus two dependent divides per channel. Replaced with a branchless AVX2 kernel using the *exact* scalar operation order for the polynomial and `blendv` selects for the early-outs (the polynomial passes exactly through (3,1)/(-3,-1) and is monotone, so the saturated lanes are bit-identical). Result is **bit-identical** to the scalar oracle.\n\n3. Both kernels hoist the loop-invariant decisions (stereo input, connected dry/wet slots) into compile-time template parameters, and mono input computes the waveshaper once and broadcasts to both output channels \u00e2\u0080\u0094 matching the scalar body's `x_r = x_l` semantics exactly.\n\n**Verification**: check confirmed allowlist + build + equality (soft bit-exact, tanh approx at -133.6 dBFS), with the benchmark showing `op_distort_tanh` **14.62\u00c3\u0097** (978.37 \u00e2\u0086\u0092 66.91 ns/block) and `op_distort_soft` **6.63\u00c3\u0097** (260.72 \u00e2\u0086\u0092 39.35 ns/block), stable across three runs (66.36/66.4/66.91 and 39.27/39.38/39.35).
